in (
5.41
), then
(x^{j}(x,v),v^{j-1}(x,v))
is non-grazing for all
j=1,2,\dots,M
. Moreover, if we put a whole travel length as
L=NT
, then

\displaystyle\inf_{1\leq i\leq M}|v^{i-1}(x,v)\cdot n(x^{i}(x,v))|\geq C^{*}_{\varepsilon,L}>0,

where the constant
C^{*}_{\varepsilon,L}
is independent of
x
.
∎

6.
Transversality and Nondegeneracy condition

In this section, we briefly explain nondegeneracy via triple Duhamel iteration technique which was constructed in
[
25
]
. In fact, Theorem
5.7
plays role of velocity lemma (see Lemma 2.7 in
[
25
]
) and guarantees uniform non-grazing with uniformly finite number of bounces away from some sets with sufficiently small measure. Instead of repeating full detail of iteration scheme in
[
25
]
again, we provide a brief scheme of the triple iteration with some modification considering Theorem
5.7
.

First, let us define local parametrization. For a given point
{p}\in\partial{\Omega}
, we choose sufficiently small
r_{1}>0,r_{2}>0
, and an bijective smooth mapping

\begin{split}\eta_{{p}}:\{\mathbf{x}_{{p}}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\mathbf{x}_{{p},3}<0\}\cap B(0;r_{1})\ &\rightarrow\ \Omega\cap B({p};r_{2}),\\
\mathbf{x}_{{p}}=(\mathbf{x}_{{p},1},\mathbf{x}_{{p},2},\mathbf{x}_{{p},3})\ &\mapsto\ (x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=\eta_{{p}}(\mathbf{x}_{{p},1},\mathbf{x}_{{p},2},\mathbf{x}_{{p},3}),\end{split}

(6.1)

where
\eta_{{{p}}}(\mathbf{x}_{{{p}},1},\mathbf{x}_{{{p}},2},\mathbf{x}_{{{p}},3})\in\partial\Omega
if and only if
\mathbf{x}_{{{p}},3}=0
. We also define the transformed velocity field at
\eta_{{p}}(\mathbf{x}_{{p}})
as

\mathbf{v}_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{{p}}):=\frac{\partial_{i}\eta_{{p}}(\mathbf{x}_{{p}})}{\sqrt{g_{{p},ii}(\mathbf{x}_{{p}})}}\cdot v,

(6.2)

where

\Big\{\frac{\partial_{1}\eta_{{p}}}{\sqrt{g_{{p},11}}},\frac{\partial_{2}\eta_{{p}}}{\sqrt{g_{{p},22}}},\frac{\partial_{3}\eta_{{p}}}{\sqrt{g_{{p},33}}}\Big\}\text{ is orthonormal at }\mathbf{x}_{{p},3}=0.

(6.3)

For the second derivative
\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\eta_{p}
, we define Christoffel symbol
\Gamma{ij}^{k}
by

\partial_{ij}\eta_{p}=\sum_{k}\Gamma_{p,ij}^{k}\partial_{k}\eta_{p}.

(6.4)

Without loss of generality, we set
g_{{p},33}(\mathbf{x}_{{p},1},\mathbf{x}_{{p},2},\mathbf{x}_{{p},3})=1
whenever it is defined. The outward normal on the boundary is, for
x=\eta_{{p}}(\mathbf{x}_{{p},1},\mathbf{x}_{{p},2},0)\in\partial\Omega
,

n(x)=n(\eta_{{p}}(\mathbf{x}_{{p},1},\mathbf{x}_{{p},2},0))=\partial_{3}\eta_{{p}}(\mathbf{x}_{{p},1},\mathbf{x}_{{p},2},0).

(6.5)

For each
k=0,1,2,3,\cdots,
we assume that
{p}^{k}\in\partial\Omega
is chosen to be close to
x^{k}
as in (
6.1
). Then, we define

\begin{split}\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}&:=(\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1},\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2},0)\ \text{
such that }\ x^{k}=\eta_{{p}^{k}}(\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}),\\
\mathbf{v}^{k}_{p^{k},i}&:=\mathbf{v}_{i}^{k}(\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k}})=\frac{\partial_{i}\eta_{{p}^{k}}(\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k}})}{\sqrt{g_{{p}^{k},ii}(\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k}})}}\cdot v^{k},\end{split}

(6.6)

and speed-direction components

|\mathbf{v}^{k}_{p^{k}}|=\sqrt{({\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1})^{2}+({\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2})^{2}+({\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},3})^{2}},\ \ \hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}=\frac{\mathbf{v}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{|\mathbf{v}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}|},\ \ \hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}=\frac{\mathbf{v}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{|\mathbf{v}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}|}.

(6.7)

Step 1
To use advantage of
\frac{dX(s)}{d|v|}
, we first study change of variable between local coordinate and speed-direction coordinate. Let us assume
x\in B(x_{i},r(x_{i},\varepsilon))\cap\overline{S_{\varphi}}
and
v\notin\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathcal{IB}}\cap\mathbb{V}_{N}
where
B(x_{i},r(x_{i},\varepsilon))
and
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathcal{IB}}
were defined in Theorem
5.7
. Then by Theorem
5.7
and (
6.6
),
|\mathbf{v}_{p^{k},3}^{k}(t,x,v)|>\delta_{2}(\varepsilon,N)>0
for all
1\leq k\leq M
for fixed
M
. (Note that we choose
M
to be the uniform number of bounce
M_{\varepsilon,NT}
what we obtained in Lemma
4.22
.) Applying Lemma 2.9 of
[
25
]
directly, we can obtain

\bigg|\det\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|cc}\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}&\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}\\
\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}&\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}\\
\hline\cr\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}&\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}\\
\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\mathbf{x}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}&\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},1}}&\frac{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2}}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{{p}^{1},2}}\end{array}\right]\bigg|\ >\ \epsilon_{\Omega,N,\delta_{2}}>0,

(6.8)

where
t^{1}=t^{1}(t,x,v),

\mathbf{x}^{1}_{p^{1},i}=\mathbf{x}^{1}_{p^{1},i}(t,x,v),

\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{p^{1},i}=\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{p^{1},i}(t,x,v)
and

\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},i}=\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},i}(t^{1},\mathbf{x}^{1}_{p^{1},1},\mathbf{x}^{1}_{p^{1},2},\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{p^{1},1},\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{p^{1},2},|\mathbf{v}^{1}_{p^{1}}|),\ \ \ \hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},i}=\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},i}(t^{1},\mathbf{x}^{1}_{p^{1},1},\mathbf{x}^{1}_{p^{1},2},\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{p^{1},1},\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{1}_{p^{1},2},|\mathbf{v}^{1}_{p^{1}}|).

Here, the constant
\epsilon_{\Omega,N,\delta_{2}}=\epsilon_{\Omega,\varepsilon,N}>0
does not depend on
t
and
x
.

Step 2
Now we study geometric decomposition for Jabocian of
X(s;t,x,v)
with respect to speed-direction coordinate. We define specular basis first.

Definition 6.1

(Specular Basis)
.

Recall the specular cycles
(t^{k},x^{k},v^{k})
in section 1.2. Assume non-grazing

n(x^{k})\cdot v^{k}\neq 0,\quad 1\leq k\leq M,

(6.9)

for each bounce we consider. Also, recall
\eta_{p^{k}}
in (
6.1
). Specular basis is an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
which is defined by

\begin{split}&\mathbf{{e}}^{k}_{0}:=\frac{v^{k}}{|v^{k}|},\\
&\mathbf{{e}}^{k}_{\perp,1}:=\mathbf{e}^{k}_{0}\times\frac{\partial_{2}\eta_{p^{k}}(x^{k})}{\sqrt{g_{{p}^{k},22}(x^{k})}}\bigg/\Big|\mathbf{e}^{k}_{0}\times\frac{\partial_{2}\eta_{p^{k}}(x^{k})}{\sqrt{g_{{p}^{k},22}(x^{k})}}\Big|,\\
&\mathbf{{e}}^{k}_{\perp,2}:=\mathbf{e}^{k}_{0}\times\mathbf{{e}}^{k}_{\perp,1},\end{split}

(6.10)

for each
k
and
(n(x^{k}),v^{k})
.

From uniform nondegeneracy (
6.8
), we study the following Jacobian with respect to speed-direction coordinate,

\det\frac{\partial X(s;t,x,v)}{\partial(|\mathbf{v}_{p^{k}}^{k}|,\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{p^{k},1}^{k},\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{p^{k},2}^{k})}.

Since perturbation of speed
|\mathbf{v}_{p^{k}}^{k}|=|v|
does not change bouncing points. Using this advantage, we can obtain (see (3.32) of
[
25
]
)

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial|v|}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}&\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial\hat{v}_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}&\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial\hat{v}_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}\\
\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial|v|}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}&\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial\hat{v}_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}&\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial\hat{v}_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}\\
\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial|v|}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}&\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial\hat{v}_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}&\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial\hat{v}_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}-(t-s)&{\bf(*)_{1,2}}\\
\hline\cr\mathbf{0}_{2,1}&{\bf(**)_{2,2}}\end{array}\right]_{3\times 3}.

To check full rank property of above matrix, we study determinant of lower right
{\bf(**)_{2,2}}
which is generated by directional derivative
\frac{\partial X(s)}{\partial\hat{\mathbf{v}}_{p^{k},i}^{k}}
. To study
{\bf(**)_{2,2}}
efficiently, we define specular matrix (Definition 3.3 in
[
25
]
).

Definition 6.2

(Specular Matrix)
.

For fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
and a
C^{1}
-map
Y:(y_{1},y_{2})\mapsto Y(y_{1},y_{2})\in\Omega
, assume (
6.9
)
with
{x}^{k}={x}^{k}(t,Y({y}_{1},{y}_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
and
{v}^{k}={v}^{k}(t,Y({y}_{1},{y}_{2}),

|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
. We define the
4\times 4
specular transition matrix
\mathcal{S}^{k,p^{k},Y}=\mathcal{S}^{k,p^{k},Y}(t,y_{1},y_{2},\\
|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
as

\mathcal{S}^{k,p^{k},Y}:=\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{k,p^{k},Y}&0_{2\times 2}\\
\hline\cr\mathcal{S}_{2}^{k,p^{k},Y}&\mathcal{S}_{3}^{k,p^{k},Y}\end{array}\right]_{4\times 4},

(6.12)

where

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{k,p^{k},Y}&:=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\partial_{1}\eta_{{p}^{k}}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,1}&\partial_{2}\eta_{{p}^{k}}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,1}\\
\partial_{1}\eta_{{p}^{k}}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,2}&\partial_{2}\eta_{{p}^{k}}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,2}\end{array}\right]_{2\times 2},\\
\mathcal{S}_{2}^{k,p^{k},Y}&:=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\partial_{1}\big[\frac{\partial_{\ell}\eta_{{p}^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{{{p}^{k}},\ell\ell}}}\big]\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{{p}^{k}},\ell}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,1}&\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\partial_{2}\big[\frac{\partial_{\ell}\eta_{{p}^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{{{p^{k}}},\ell\ell}}}\big]\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{{p}},\ell}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}\\
\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\partial_{1}\big[\frac{\partial_{\ell}\eta_{{p^{k}}}}{\sqrt{g_{{{p^{k}}},\ell\ell}}}\big]\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{{p^{k}}},\ell}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,2}&\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\partial_{2}\big[\frac{\partial_{\ell}\eta_{{p^{k}}}}{\sqrt{g_{{{p^{k}}},\ell\ell}}}\big]\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{{p^{k}}},\ell}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,2}\end{array}\right]_{2\times 2},\\
\mathcal{S}_{3}^{k,p^{k},Y}&:=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big[\frac{\partial_{1}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},11}}}-\frac{\partial_{3}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},33}}}\frac{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},1}}{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},3}}\Big]\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,1}&\Big[\frac{\partial_{2}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},22}}}-\frac{\partial_{3}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},33}}}\frac{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},2}}{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},3}}\Big]\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,1}\\
\Big[\frac{\partial_{1}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},11}}}-\frac{\partial_{3}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},33}}}\frac{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},1}}{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},3}}\Big]\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,2}&\Big[\frac{\partial_{2}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},22}}}-\frac{\partial_{3}\eta_{p^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{p^{k},33}}}\frac{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},2}}{\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{p^{k},3}}\Big]\cdot\mathbf{e}^{k}_{\perp,2}\end{array}\right]_{2\times 2},\end{split}

where
\eta_{p^{k}}
and
g_{p^{k}}
are evaluated at
x^{k}(t,Y({y}_{1},{y}_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
. We also define the
4\times 4
specular matrix
\mathcal{R}^{k,p^{k},Y}=\mathcal{R}^{k,p^{k},Y}(t,y_{1},y_{2},|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
as

\mathcal{R}^{k,p^{k},Y}:=\mathcal{S}^{k,p^{k},Y}\frac{\partial(\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1},\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},2},\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1},\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},1})}{\partial(y_{1},y_{2},\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})},

(6.13)

where
\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}=\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}(t,Y({y}_{1},{y}_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
,
\mathbf{v}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}=\mathbf{v}^{k}_{{p}^{k}}(t,Y({y}_{1},{y}_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
. Here,
\hat{v}_{i}
is directional component in global Eulerian coordinate
\hat{v}_{i}=\frac{v_{i}}{|v|}
.

Once we perform triple iteration, we can generate extra variables
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
. Explicitly, we have

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}&\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}&\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}\end{array}\right]^{-1}\frac{\partial X(s;t,Y_{x}(y_{1},y_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})}{\partial(|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2},y_{1},y_{2})}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\frac{\partial X}{\partial|v|}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial\hat{v}_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial\hat{v}_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial y_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial y_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}\\
\frac{\partial X}{\partial|v|}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial\hat{v}_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial\hat{v}_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial y_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial y_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,1}^{k}\\
\frac{\partial X}{\partial|v|}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial\hat{v}_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial\hat{v}_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial y_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}&\frac{\partial X}{\partial y_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\perp,2}^{k}\end{array}\right]}.

Using the
specular basis
(
6.10
) and the
specular matrix
(
6.13
), the underbraced term becomes

\displaystyle{\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}-(t-s)&\begin{subarray}{c}-|{v}^{k}|\nabla_{\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2},y_{1},y_{2}}t^{k}+\nabla_{\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2},y_{1},y_{2}}\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},\ell}\partial_{\ell}\eta_{{p}^{k}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{0}^{k}\\
-(t^{k}-s)|{v}^{k}|\sum_{j=1}^{2}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},j}}}\Big[\frac{\partial_{\ell}\eta_{{p}^{k}}}{\sqrt{g_{{p}^{k},\ell\ell}}}\Big]\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k}_{{p}^{k},\ell}\bigg)\nabla_{\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2},y_{1},y_{2}}\mathbf{x}^{k}_{{p}^{k},j}\end{subarray}\\
\hline\cr\begin{subarray}{c}0\\
0\end{subarray}&*_{2\times 4}\end{array}\right]_{3\times 5}},

where the lower right
2\times 4
-submatrix equals

\begin{split}&\left[\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathcal{R}_{1,1}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{1,2}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{1,3}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{1,4}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}\\
\mathcal{R}_{2,1}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{2,2}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{2,3}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{2,4}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}\end{array}\right]\\
&-(t^{k}-s)|{v}^{k}|\left[\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathcal{R}_{3,1}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{3,2}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{3,3}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{3,4}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}\\
\mathcal{R}_{4,1}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{4,2}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{4,3}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}&\mathcal{R}_{4,4}^{k,{p}^{k},Y_{x}}\end{array}\right].\end{split}

(6.17)

Here
\mathcal{R}^{k,p^{k},Y_{x}}_{i,j}
is defined in (
6.13
) with
x^{k}=x^{k}(t,Y_{x}(y_{1},y_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
and
v^{k}=v^{k}(t,Y_{x}

(y_{1},y_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})
. From the exact form of underbraced submatrix, choosing
2\times 2
submatrix in the underbraced matrix is equivalent to choosing two independent variables
\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}
among
\{\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2},y_{1},y_{2}\}
. (Here,
(y_{1},y_{2})
is introduced to represent a local spatial coordinate of a manifold which is constructed by
\partial_{|v|},\partial_{\hat{v}_{i}}
in its second Duhamel iteration step.)

Step 3
From the existence of
\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}\subset\{\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2},y_{1},y_{2}\}
which gives nondegeneracy of
2\times 2
submatrix of (
6.17
), we can prove full rank property.
We recall Lemm 3.7 of
[
25
]
. Away from arbitrary small time intervals near
C^{1}
function
\psi_{i}^{k}:B_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
(for
i=1,2,3
), we obtain nondegeneracy. The following Lemma is same as Lemma 3.7 in
[
25
]
. We obtain the following Lemma by replacing
\mathbf{e}_{3}
into
\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)
WLOG.

Lemma 6.3
.

[
S_{\varphi}
version for Lemma 3.7 in
[
25
]
]
Let
x\in S_{\varphi}
. We also assume at most
M
bounces during
[t-1,t]
and

\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}\leq|v|\leq N,\ \frac{1}{N}\leq|v\cdot\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)|,\ \frac{1}{N}\leq|n(x^{1}(t,x,v))\cdot\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)|,\\
\text{and}\ {|n(x^{k}(t,x,v))\cdot v^{k}(t,x,v)|}\geq\delta_{1}>0,\quad 1\leq k\leq M.\end{split}

(6.18)

where
\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)
is a unit vector that is perpendicular to the cross-section
S_{\varphi}
. Let a
C^{1}
-map
Y_{x}:(y_{1},y_{2})\mapsto Y_{x}(y_{1},y_{2})\in\bar{\Omega}
with
Y_{x}(0,0)=x
and
\|Y\|_{C^{1}_{x,y_{1},y_{2}}}\lesssim 1
. We assume that

\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial Y_{x}(0,0)}{\partial y_{1}}\times\frac{\partial Y_{x}(0,0)}{\partial y_{2}}\Big)\cdot R_{x^{1}(t,x,v)}v^{1}(t,x,v)\Big|>\delta_{3}>0.

(6.19)

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
t^{k}\geq t-1
, there exists
\varepsilon>0
and finitely many
C^{0,\gamma}
-functions
\psi^{k}_{i}:B_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
\|\psi^{k}_{i}\|_{C^{0,\gamma}_{t,x}}\lesssim 1
, and there exists a constant
\epsilon_{\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N,\Omega,\delta_{*}}>0
and
\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}\subset\{\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2},y_{1},y_{2}\}
such that

\displaystyle\text{if }\ \min_{i}|s-\psi_{i}^{k}(t,Y_{x}(y_{1},y_{2}),v)|>\delta_{*}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \text{and}\ (s;t,Y_{x}(y_{1},y_{2}),v)\in[\max\{t-1,t^{k+1}\},\min\{t-\frac{1}{N},t^{k}\}]\times B_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v),

\displaystyle\text{then}\ \det\left(\frac{\partial X(s;t,Y_{x}(y_{1},y_{2}),|v|,\hat{v}_{1},\hat{v}_{2})}{\partial(|v|,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})}\right)>\epsilon_{\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N,\Omega,\delta_{*}}>0.

Before we apply above Lemma to the following Proposition, we again note that the second and third conditions of (
6.18
) are written as

\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}\leq|v_{3}|,\quad\frac{1}{N}\leq|n(x^{1}(t,x,v))\cdot e_{3}|,\end{split}

in
[
25
]
, where the choice of
e_{3}
can be changed into any other fixed direction vector in
\mathbb{S}^{2}
WLOG. Since we are dealing with
x\in S_{\varphi}
, we choose
\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)
instead of
e_{3}
which is more suitable in our domain.

Modifying Theorem 3.9 of
[
25
]
into
\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}
version (
\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}
is obtained in Theorem
5.7
), we obtain the following Proposition.

Proposition 6.4
.

[
\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}
version for Theorem 3.9 in
[
25
]
]
Fix any arbitrary
(t,x,v)\in[T,T+1]\times\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{V}^{N}
. There exists
\delta_{2}>0
and
C^{1}
-function
\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k}
for
k\leq M
where
\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k}
is defined locally around
(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0},X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell})
with
(\ell_{0},\vec{\ell})=(\ell_{0},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3})\in\{0,1,\cdots,\lfloor\frac{1}{\delta_{2}}\rfloor+1\}\times\{-\lfloor\frac{N}{\delta_{2}}\rfloor-1,\cdots,0,\cdots,\lfloor\frac{N}{\delta_{2}}\rfloor+1\}^{3}
and
\|\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k}\|_{C^{1}}\leq C_{N,\Omega,\varepsilon,\delta_{2}}<\infty
.

Moreover, if

(X(s;t,x,v),u)\in\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon},

(6.20)

(s,u)\in[T+(\ell_{0}-1)\delta_{2},T+(\ell_{0}+1)\delta_{2}]\times B(\delta_{2}\vec{\ell};2\delta_{2}),

(6.21)

s^{\prime}\in\big[t^{k+1}(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell})+\frac{1}{N},\ t^{k}(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell})-\frac{1}{N}\big],

(6.22)

and

|s^{\prime}-\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k}(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0},X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell})|>N^{2}(1+\|\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k}\|_{C^{1}})\delta_{2},

(6.23)

then

\big|\partial_{|u|}X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)\times\partial_{\hat{u}_{1}}X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)\big|>\epsilon_{\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta_{2}}.

(6.24)

Here
\epsilon_{\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta_{2}}>0
does not depend on
T,t,x,v
.

There exists
\delta_{3}>0
and
C^{1}
-functions

\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k,m_{0},\vec{m},k^{\prime}}_{1},\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k,m_{0},\vec{m},k^{\prime}}_{2},\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k,m_{0},\vec{m},k^{\prime}}_{3},

(6.25)

for
k^{\prime}\leq M
where
\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k,m_{0},\vec{m},k^{\prime}}_{n}
is defined locally around
(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};X(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0},X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell}),\delta_{3}\vec{m})
for some
(m_{0},\vec{m})=(m_{0},m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\in\{0,1,\cdots,\lfloor\frac{1}{\delta_{3}}\rfloor+1\}\times\{-\lfloor\frac{N}{\delta_{3}}\rfloor-1,\cdots,0,\cdots,\lfloor\frac{N}{\delta_{3}}\rfloor+1\}^{3}
with
0<\delta_{3}\ll 1
.

Moreover, if we assume (
6.20
), (
6.21
), (
6.22
), (
6.23
),

\begin{split}&(X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),u^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon},\ \text{and}\\
&\quad\quad\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial_{y_{1}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)\times\partial_{y_{2}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)}{|\partial_{y_{1}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)\times\partial_{y_{2}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)|}\Big)\cdot u^{\prime}\Big|>\frac{1}{N}>0,\end{split}

(6.26)

\begin{split}s^{\prime\prime}\in&\ \Big[t^{k^{\prime}+1}(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};X(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0},X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell}),\delta_{3}\vec{m})+\frac{1}{N}\\
&\ \ \ \ ,t^{k^{\prime}}(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};X(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0},X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell}),\delta_{3}\vec{m})-\frac{1}{N}\Big],\end{split}

(6.27)

and

\begin{split}&\min_{n=1,2,3}|s^{\prime\prime}-\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k,m_{0},\vec{m},k^{\prime}}_{n}(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};X(T+\delta_{3}m_{0};T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0},X(T+\delta_{2}\ell_{0};t,x,v),\delta_{2}\vec{\ell}),\delta_{3}\vec{m})|\\
&>N^{2}(1+\max_{n=1,2,3}\|\psi^{\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k,m_{0},\vec{m},k^{\prime}}_{n}\|_{C^{1}})(\delta_{3}),\end{split}

(6.28)

then for each
\ell_{0},\vec{\ell},k,m_{0},\vec{m},k^{\prime}
we can choose two distinct variables
\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}\subset\{|u|,\hat{u}_{1},\hat{u}_{1}^{\prime},\hat{u}_{2}^{\prime}\}
such that
(|u^{\prime}|,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\mapsto X(s^{\prime\prime};s^{\prime},X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),u^{\prime})
is one-to-one locally and

\Big|\det\left(\frac{\partial X(s^{\prime\prime};s^{\prime},X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),u^{\prime})}{\partial(|u^{\prime}|,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})}\right)\Big|>\epsilon^{\prime}_{\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta_{2},\delta_{3}}.

(6.29)

Here
\epsilon^{\prime}_{\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta_{2},\delta_{3}}>0
does not depend on
T,t,x,v
.

Proof.

Main proof is nearly the same as the proof of Theorem 3.9 in
[
25
]
. Proof of Theorem 3.9 in
[
25
]
comes from Lemma 3.7 in
[
25
]
. Similarly, Proposition
6.4
can be proved using Lemma
6.3
. Instead of providing full proof, we just explain why it is possible to apply Lemma
6.3
and Theorem
5.7
.

Let us combine Theorem
5.7
and Lemma
6.3
. First, when we assume Theorem
5.7
, we should check that
(x,v)\in\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}

satisfies (
6.18
) in Lemma
6.3
. The first and fourth conditions of (
6.18
) hold obviously by the statement of Theorem
5.7
, by the definition of
\mathbb{V}^{N}
and choosing
\delta_{1}=C^{*}_{\varepsilon,L}
of Theorem
5.7
. Let us check the second and third conditions.

For the second condition, we recall Lemma
4.16
in which we excluded
R_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
. If we consider Lemma
4.16
in general
S_{\varphi}
cross-section, excluding
R_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
exactly corresponds to
\frac{1}{N}\leq|v\cdot\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)|
for some proper
\varepsilon\ll 1
and
N\gg 1
. The third condition of (
6.18
) is also similar because it is easy to observe

|v\cdot\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)|=0\Longleftrightarrow|n(x^{1}(t,x,v))\cdot\hat{\varphi}(\varphi)|=0,\quad x\in\overline{S_{\varphi}},

by
z
-axisymmetric structure. In conclusion,
(x,v)\in\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}
satisfies all conditions in (
6.18
).

Now, above argument is applied to
(X(s;t,x,v),u)\in\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}
and
(X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)

,u^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}
by (
6.20
) and (
6.26
). Moreover, condition (
6.26
) implies (
6.19
) putting
x^{0}=X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)
. Therefore, the proof of Theorem 3.9 of
[
25
]
can be directly applied to prove Proposition
6.4
. Note that
\delta_{1}
of Lemma
6.3
is chosen by
\delta_{1}=C^{*}_{\varepsilon,L}
by Theorem
5.7
. So choosing
L=NT_{0}=N
(we choose time interval
T_{0}=1
here), we get the dependence of
\epsilon^{\prime}_{\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta_{2},\delta_{3}}
. We finish the proof.
∎

7.
Triple Duhamel iterations

Note that the kernel
\mathbf{k}(v,u)
satisfies (see
[
16
,
12
]
)

\mathbf{k}(v,u)\lesssim\big(\frac{1}{|v-u|}+|v-u|\big)e^{-\frac{1}{8}|v-u|^{2}-\frac{1}{8}\frac{||v|^{2}-|u|^{2}|^{2}}{|v-u|^{2}}}.

Now we can write an equation for
f
from (
1.1
) and (
1.16
):

Motivated by characteristic
(X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v))
, we define mild solution of (
1.21
):

\begin{split}f(t,x,v)&=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\nu(V(\tau;t,x,v))d\tau}f_{0}(X(0;t,x,v),V(0;t,x,v))\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{s}^{t}\nu(V(\tau;t,x,v))d\tau}\int_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}k(V(s;t,x,v),u)f(s,X(s;t,x,v),u)duds\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{s}^{t}\nu(V(\tau;t,x,v))d\tau}\Gamma(f,f)(s,X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v))ds.\\
\end{split}

(7.1)

In this section, we also briefly explain triple Duhamel expansion which was explained in
[
25
]
.
First, let
f
solves linearized Boltzmann equation (
7.2
)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla f+\nu f=Kf,\quad f(t,x,v)=f(t,x,R_{x}v)\ \ \text{on $x\in\partial\Omega$}.\end{split}

(7.2)

Let
h:=wf
where
w=(1+|v|)^{\beta},\ \beta>5/2
. Then the linearized Boltzmann equation becomes

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}h+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\nu h=K_{w}h,\quad K_{w}h:=wK(\frac{h}{w}).\end{split}

Let us define,

\begin{split}E(v,t,s)&:=\exp\Big\{-\int_{s}^{t}\nu(V(\tau;t,x,v))\Big\}.\\
\end{split}

Along the backward in time trajectory,

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{ds}\Big(E(v,t,s)h(s,X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v))\Big)\\
&={E(v,t,s)}\big[K_{w}h\big](s,X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v)).\end{split}

By integrating from
0
to
t
, we obtain the first Duhamel expansion,

\begin{split}h(t,x,v)=&E(v,t,0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}k_{w}(u,V(s))h(s,X(s;t,x,v),u)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

(7.3)

where we abbreviated
X(s)=X(s;t,x,v)
. Recall the standard estimates (see Lemma 4 and Lemma 5 in
[
19
]
)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|k_{w}(v,u)|\mathrm{d}u\leq C_{K}\langle v\rangle^{-1}.

(7.4)

From double and triple iteration, we get

\displaystyle h(t,x,v)

(7.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E(v,t,0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u}k_{w}(u,V(s))E(u,s,0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))duds

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u}k_{w}(u,V(s))\int^{s}_{0}E(u,s,s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime}}k_{w}(u^{\prime},V(s^{\prime}))h(s^{\prime},X(s^{\prime}),u^{\prime})du^{\prime}ds^{\prime}duds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E(v,t,0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u}k_{w}(u,V(s))E(u,s,0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))duds

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u}k_{w}(u,V(s))\int^{s}_{0}E(u,s,s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime}}k_{w}(u^{\prime},V(s^{\prime}))E(u^{\prime},s^{\prime},0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))du^{\prime}ds^{\prime}duds

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u}k_{w}(u,V(s))\int^{s}_{0}E(u,s,s^{\prime})\int_{u^{\prime}}k_{w}(u^{\prime},V(s^{\prime}))E(u^{\prime},s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\quad\quad

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime\prime}}k_{w}(u^{\prime\prime},V(s^{\prime\prime}))h(s^{\prime\prime},X(s^{\prime\prime}),u^{\prime\prime})du^{\prime\prime}ds^{\prime\prime}du^{\prime}ds^{\prime}duds,

where we abbreviated notations (similar for
V
),

\begin{split}X(s)&:=X(s;t,x,v),\quad X(s^{\prime}):=X^{\prime}(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),\\
X(s^{\prime\prime})&:=X(s^{\prime\prime};s^{\prime},X^{\prime}(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),u^{\prime}).\end{split}

(7.6)

Now, we choose
m({N})
so that

k_{w,m}(u,v):=\mathbf{1}_{\{|u-v|\geq\frac{1}{m},\ |u|\leq m\}}k_{w}(u,v),

(7.7)

satisfies
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|k_{w,m}(u,v)-k_{w}(u,v)|\ \mathrm{d}u\leq\frac{1}{{N}}
for sufficiently large
{N}\geq 1
. From (
7.7
) and uniform lower bound of collision frequency, we can rewrite (
7.5
) as

\displaystyle h(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=E(v,t,0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u,V(s))E(u,s,0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))duds

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u,V(s))\int^{s}_{0}E(u,s,s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime}}k_{w}(u^{\prime},V(s^{\prime}))E(u^{\prime},s^{\prime},0)h_{0}(X(0),V(0))du^{\prime}ds^{\prime}duds

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u}k_{w,m}(u,V(s))\int^{s}_{0}E(u,s,s^{\prime})\int_{u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime},V(s^{\prime}))E(u^{\prime},s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime\prime},V(s^{\prime\prime}))h(s^{\prime\prime},X(s^{\prime\prime}),u^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+O_{\Omega}(\frac{1}{N})\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|h(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{\nu_{0}}{2}t}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+O_{\Omega}(\frac{1}{N})\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|h(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{t}_{0}E(v,t,s)\int_{u}k_{w,m}(u,V(s))\int^{s}_{0}E(u,s,s^{\prime})\int_{u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime},V(s^{\prime}))E(u^{\prime},s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime\prime},V(s^{\prime\prime}))h(s^{\prime\prime},X(s^{\prime\prime}),u^{\prime\prime}).

(7.8)

Let us analyze (
7.8
).
We define following sets for fixed
\delta,n,\vec{n},k,m,\vec{m},k^{\prime}
, where Proposition
6.4
does not hold,

\begin{split}R_{1}&:=\{u\ |\ (X(s;t,x,v),u)\notin\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}\ \text{and}\ u\notin B(\vec{n}\delta;2\delta)\},\\
R_{2}&:=\{s^{\prime}\ |\ |s-s^{\prime}|\leq\delta\},\\
R_{3}&:=\{s^{\prime}\ |\ |s^{\prime}-\psi_{1}^{n,\vec{n},k,m,\vec{m},k^{\prime}}(n\delta,X(n\delta;t,x,v),\vec{n}\delta)|\lesssim_{N}\delta\|\psi_{1}\|_{C^{1}}\},\\
R_{4}&:=\{u^{\prime}\ |\ (X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),u^{\prime})\notin\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}\ \text{and}\ u^{\prime}\notin B(\vec{m}\delta;2\delta)\},\\
R_{5}&:=\{u^{\prime}\ |\ \Big|\Big(\frac{\partial_{y_{1}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)\times\partial_{y_{2}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)}{|\partial_{y_{1}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)\times\partial_{y_{2}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)|}\Big)\cdot u^{\prime}\Big|\leq\delta\},\\
R_{6}&:=\{s^{\prime\prime}\ |\ |s^{\prime}-s^{\prime\prime}|\leq\delta\},\\
R_{7}&:=\{s^{\prime\prime}\ |\ \min_{r=1,2}|s^{\prime\prime}-\psi_{r}^{n,\vec{n},k,m,\vec{m},k^{\prime}}(m\delta,X(m\delta;n\delta,X(n\delta;t,x,v),\vec{m}\delta),\vec{n}\delta)|\lesssim_{N}\delta\min_{r=1,2}\|\psi_{r}\|_{C^{1}}\}.\\
\end{split}

Therefore, (
7.8
) can be controlled by

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{[t/\delta]+1}\sum_{|\vec{n}|\leq N}\sum_{m=0}^{[t/\delta]+1}\sum_{|\vec{m}|\leq N}\sum_{k=1}^{M}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{M}\int^{(n+1)\delta}_{(n-1)\delta}\ \int^{t^{k}-\delta}_{t^{k+1}+\delta}\int^{t^{k^{\prime}}-\delta}_{t^{k^{\prime}+1}+\delta}e^{-{\nu_{0}}(t-s^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{u,u^{\prime},u^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}\ |h(s^{\prime\prime},X(s^{\prime\prime}),u^{\prime\prime})|\ \mathbf{1}_{R_{1}^{c}\cap R_{2}^{c}\cap R_{3}^{c}\cap R_{4}^{c}\cap R_{5}^{c}\cap R_{6}^{c}}

(7.9)

\displaystyle\quad+B+R.

Here
B
term corresponds to when the trajectory locates near bouncing points and
R
corresponds to where
(u,s^{\prime},u^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})
belong to at least one of
R_{1}\sim R_{7}
. Exact definition and estimates for
B
and
R
are given as following

\begin{split}B&\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{0}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-{\nu_{0}}(t-s^{\prime\prime})}\int_{u\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u,v)\int_{u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime},u)\\
&\quad\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime})h(s^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime}(s^{\prime\prime}),u^{\prime\prime})\mathbf{1}_{|s^{\prime}-t^{k}|\leq\delta\ \text{or}\ |s^{\prime\prime}-t^{k^{\prime}}|\leq\delta}\\
&\leq C_{N}\delta\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|h(s)\|_{\infty},\\
R&\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{0}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-{\nu_{0}}(t-s^{\prime\prime})}\int_{u\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u,v)\int_{u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime},u)\\
&\quad\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{V}^{N}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime})h(s^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime}(s^{\prime\prime}),u^{\prime\prime})\ \mathbf{1}_{R_{1}\cup R_{2}\cup R_{3}\cup R_{4}\cup R_{5}\cup R_{6}\cup R_{7}}\\
&\leq C_{N}\delta\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|h(s)\|_{\infty}.\\
\end{split}

(7.10)

In particular, for about
R
estimate, deriving smallness
(\delta+\varepsilon)
from
R_{2},R_{3},R_{5},R_{6},R_{7}
is easy. To derive smallness from
R_{1}
and
R_{4}
, we use (
5.42
) in Theorem
5.7
to obtain

\int_{u}k_{w,m}(u,V(s))\mathbf{1}_{R_{1}}\lesssim\delta,\quad\int_{u^{\prime}}k_{w,m}(u^{\prime},V(s^{\prime}))\mathbf{1}_{R_{4}}\lesssim\delta,

choosing
\varepsilon
sufficiently small depending on
N\gg 1
in (
5.42
).

For (
7.9
), we are away from two sets
B
and
R
. Under the condition of
(u,s^{\prime},u^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\in R_{1}^{c}\ \cap\ R_{2}^{c}\ \cap R_{3}^{c}\ \cap R_{4}^{c}\ \cap R_{5}^{c}\ \cap R_{6}^{c}\ \cap R_{7}^{c}
, indices
n,\vec{n},k,m,\vec{m},k^{\prime}
are determined so that

\begin{split}t\ &\in\ [(n-1)\delta,(n+1)\delta],\\
(X(s;t,x,v),u)\ &\in\ \mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon},\\
u\ &\in\ B(\vec{n}\delta;2\delta),\\
(X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),u^{\prime})\ &\in\ \mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon},\\
u^{\prime}\ &\in\ B(\vec{m}\delta;2\delta),\\
\Big|\Big(\frac{\partial_{y_{1}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)\times\partial_{y_{2}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)}{|\partial_{y_{1}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)\times\partial_{y_{2}}Y_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}(0,0)|}\Big)\cdot u^{\prime}\Big|&\geq\delta.\end{split}

(7.11)

We can apply Proposition
6.4
which gives local time-independent lower bound of

\Big|\det(\frac{\partial(X(s^{\prime\prime}))}{\partial(|u^{\prime}|,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})})\Big|\ \geq\ \epsilon^{\prime}_{\delta}.

Note that
\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}\subset\{|u|,\hat{u}_{1},\hat{u}^{\prime}_{1},\hat{u}^{\prime}_{2}\}
are chosen variables in Theorem
6.4
and
\{\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4}\}\subset\{|u|,\hat{u}_{1},\hat{u}^{\prime}_{1},\hat{u}^{\prime}_{2}\}
are unchosen variables. Let us use
\mathcal{P}
to denote projection of

B(\vec{n}\delta;2\delta)\cap(\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon})_{X(s;t,x,v)}\times B(\vec{m}\delta;2\delta)\cap(\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon})_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)},

into
\mathbb{R}^{3}
which corresponds to
(|u^{\prime}|,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
components. Note that both
(\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon})_{X(s;t,x,v)}
and
(\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon})_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}
are defined using definition (
4.46
). If we choose sufficiently small
\delta
, there exist small
r_{\delta,n,\vec{n},k,m,\vec{m},k^{\prime}}
such that there exist one-to-one map
\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\Big(B(\vec{n}\delta;2\delta)\cap(\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon})_{X(s;t,x,v)}\times B(\vec{m}\delta;2\delta)\cap(\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon})_{X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\mapsto B(X(s^{\prime\prime};s^{\prime},X(s^{\prime};s,X(s;t,x,v),u),u^{\prime}),r_{\delta,n,\vec{n},k,m,\vec{m},k^{\prime}}).

Now, we perform change of variable for in (
7.9
) to obtain

\begin{split}\text{\eqref{main2}}&\leq\sum_{n=0}^{[t/\delta]+1}\sum_{|\vec{n}|\leq N}\sum_{m=0}^{[t/\delta]+1}\sum_{|\vec{m}|\leq N}\sum_{k=1}^{M}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{M}\int^{(n+1)\delta}_{(n-1)\delta}\ \int^{t^{k}-\delta}_{t^{k+1}+\delta}\int^{t^{k^{\prime}}-\delta}_{t^{k^{\prime}+1}}e^{-{\nu_{0}}(t-s^{\prime\prime})}\\
&\quad\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}\int_{\hat{u}_{2},\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4}}\ \mathbf{1}_{|u|\leq N,|u^{\prime}|\leq N,|u^{\prime\prime}|\leq N}\ \mathrm{d}\hat{u}_{2}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{3}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{4}\\
&\quad\quad\times\int_{|u^{\prime}|,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\ \mathrm{d}|u^{\prime}|\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}\ |h(s^{\prime\prime},X(s^{\prime\prime}),u^{\prime\prime})|\ \mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\\
&\leq\sum_{n=0}^{[t/\delta]+1}\sum_{|\vec{n}|\leq N}\sum_{m=0}^{[t/\delta]+1}\sum_{|\vec{m}|\leq N}\sum_{k=1}^{M}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{M}\int^{(n+1)\delta}_{(n-1)\delta}\ \int^{t^{k}-\delta}_{t^{k+1}+\delta}\int^{t^{k^{\prime}}-\delta}_{t^{k^{\prime}+1}+\delta}e^{-{\nu_{0}}(t-s^{\prime\prime})}\\
&\quad\quad\times\int_{\hat{u}_{2},\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4}}\ \mathbf{1}_{|u|\leq N,|u^{\prime}|\leq N,|u^{\prime\prime}|\leq N}\ \mathrm{d}\hat{u}_{2}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{3}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{4}\\
&\quad\quad\times\int_{u^{\prime\prime}}\int_{B(X(s^{\prime\prime}),r_{\delta,n,\vec{n},i,k,m,\vec{m},j,k^{\prime}})}\ |h(s^{\prime\prime},x,u^{\prime\prime})|\ \frac{1}{\epsilon^{\prime}_{\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\\
&\leq\ C_{\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-{\nu_{0}}(t-s^{\prime\prime})}\ \int_{\Omega}\int_{|u^{\prime\prime}|\leq N}|h(s^{\prime\prime},x,u^{\prime\prime})|\ \mathrm{d}u^{\prime\prime}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\\
&\leq\ C_{t,\Omega,N,\varepsilon,\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\|h(s)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}ds.\\
\end{split}

(7.12)

We collect (
7.12
), (
7.9
), (
7.10
), and choose sufficiently large
N
and (corresponding) sufficiently small
\varepsilon,\delta
to obtain

\|h(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim e^{-\frac{\nu_{0}}{2}t}\|h(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C_{t}\int_{0}^{t}\|h(s)\|_{L^{2}}ds.

(7.13)

8.
Linear and nonlinear decay : Proof of main theorem

This part is also standard and similar as
[
26
]
and
[
16
]
. Let us briefly explain key steps. We first start with linear
L^{2}_{x,v}
decay.

8.1.
Linear
L^{2}
decay

We use a standard coercivity estimate (Proposition 1.4 in
[
25
]
):

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
f
solves linearized Boltzmann equation (
7.2
) with the specular BC. Furthermore, we assume (
1.46
). Then there exists
C>0
such that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\int^{N+1}_{N}\|\mathbf{P}f(t)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq C\int^{N+1}_{N}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f(t)\|_{\nu}^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

(8.1)

Remark 8.2
.

In
[
16
]
, Guo obtained the coercivity estimate (
8.1
) by using contradiction method. When we consider a periodic cylindrical domain with an annulus cross-section, we derive the same coercivity estimate through a constructive method in Appendix, Proposition
9.6
.

Corollary 8.3
.

Assume the same conditions of Proposition
8.1
. Then we have following estimate

\sup_{0\leq t}e^{\lambda t}\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},

(8.2)

for some
\lambda>0
.

Proof.

From Green’s identity (or
L^{2}
energy estimate) for (
7.2
) in a time interval
[0,N]
,

\|f(N)\|_{2}^{2}+\int^{N}_{0}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}fLf\leq\|f(0)\|_{2}^{2}.

From (
7.2
), for any
\lambda>0
, we get

\big[\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big](e^{\lambda t}f)+L(e^{\lambda t}f)=\lambda e^{\lambda t}f,

Green’s identity (or
L^{2}
energy estimate) yields

\|e^{\lambda t}f(N)\|_{2}^{2}+\underbrace{\int^{N}_{0}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{2\lambda s}fLf}_{(I)}-{\lambda\int^{N}_{0}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|e^{\lambda s}f(s)|^{2}}\leq\|f(0)\|_{2}^{2}.

(8.3)

Let us consider
(I)
in (
8.3
). From semi-positivity of linearized Boltzmann operator
L
,
(I)
is bounded from below as

\displaystyle(I)\geq\delta_{L}\int^{N}_{0}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle v\rangle|e^{\lambda s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f|^{2}\geq\delta_{L}\int^{N}_{0}\|e^{\lambda s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{\nu}^{2}.

By time translation, we apply coercivity estimate (
8.1
) to obtain

\begin{split}(I)&\geq\frac{\delta_{L}}{2}\int^{N}_{0}\|e^{\lambda s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{\nu}^{2}+\frac{\delta_{L}}{2C}\int^{N}_{0}\|e^{\lambda s}\mathbf{P}f\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta_{L}}{2C}\int^{N}_{0}\|e^{\lambda s}f\|_{2}^{2},\end{split}

where
\|\cdot\|_{\nu}=\|\cdot\sqrt{\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
. Therefore, we derive

e^{2\lambda N}\|f(N)\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(\frac{\delta_{L}}{2C}-\lambda\Big)\int^{N}_{0}\|e^{\lambda s}f(s)\|_{2}^{2}ds\leq\|f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

(8.4)

For a time interval
[N,t]
, it is not hard to get

\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|f(N)\|_{2}^{2}.

(8.5)

Choosing
\lambda\ll 1
, from (
8.4
) and (
8.5
), we conclude that

e^{\lambda t}\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2}=e^{\lambda(t-N)}e^{\lambda N}\|f(N)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|f_{0}\|_{2}^{2},

and obtain (
8.2
). ∎

8.2.
Nonlinear
L^{\infty}
decay

Now, we are ready to derive nonlinear
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
decay from previous
L^{2}_{x,v}
decay.

Proof of Theorem
1.8
.

From (
7.13
), for
T\leq t<T+1
,

\begin{split}\sup_{s\in[T,t]}\|h(s)\|_{\infty}&\lesssim\ e^{-\nu_{0}(t-T)}\|h(T)\|_{\infty}+\int_{T}^{t}\|f(s)\|_{2}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

We assume that
m\leq t<m+1
and define
\lambda^{*}:=\min\{\nu_{0},\lambda\}
, where
\lambda
is some constant from Corollary
8.3
. We use (
7.13
) repeatedly for each time step,
[k,k+1),\ k\in\mathbb{N}
and Corollary
8.3
to perform
L^{2}-L^{\infty}
bootstrap,

\begin{split}\|h(t)\|_{\infty}&\lesssim e^{-m\nu_{0}}\|h(0)\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}e^{-k\nu_{0}}\int_{m-1-k}^{m-k}\|f(s)\|\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim e^{-m\nu_{0}}\|h(0)\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}e^{-k\nu_{0}}\int_{m-1-k}^{m-k}e^{-\lambda(m-1-k)}\|f(0)\|\mathrm{d}s\lesssim e^{-\lambda^{*}t}\|h(0)\|_{\infty}.\\
\end{split}

For nonlinear problem from Duhamel principle,

\begin{split}h&:=U(t)h_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}U(t-s)w\Gamma(\frac{h}{w},\frac{h}{w})(s)\mathrm{d}s,\\
\|h(t)\|_{\infty}&\lesssim e^{-\lambda^{*}t}\|h(0)\|_{\infty}+\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}U(t-s)w\Gamma\big(\frac{h}{w},\frac{h}{w}\big)(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{\infty},\end{split}

(8.6)

where
U(t)
is a semi-group for linearized Boltzmann equation. Using Duhamel’s principle again, we have

U(t-s)=G(t-s)+\int_{s}^{t}G(t-s_{1})K_{w}U(s_{1}-s)\mathrm{d}s_{1},

where
G(t)
is semi-group for the system

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}h+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\nu h=0,\quad\text{and}\quad|G(t)h_{0}|\leq e^{-\nu_{0}t}|h_{0}|.\end{split}

For the last term in (
8.6
), using standard nonlinear estimate
|w\Gamma(f,f)|\lesssim\langle v\rangle\|wf\|_{\infty}^{2}
,
we obtain (see
[
16
,
26
,
25
]
for detail)

\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}U(t-s)w\Gamma\big(\frac{h}{w},\frac{h}{w}\big)(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}G(t-s)w\Gamma\big(\frac{h}{w},\frac{h}{w}\big)(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{\infty}+\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}G(t-s_{1})K_{w}U(s_{1}-s)w\Gamma\big(\frac{h}{w},\frac{h}{w}\big)(s)\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq Ce^{-\lambda^{*}t}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq\infty}e^{\lambda^{*}s}\|h(s)\|_{\infty}\Big)^{2}.\end{split}

Therefore, for sufficiently small
\|h_{0}\|_{\infty}\ll 1
, we have uniform bound

\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}e^{\lambda^{*}t}\|h(t)\|_{\infty}\ll 1,

and hence global decay and uniqueness. Also note that positivity of
F
is standard by sequence
F^{\ell}
which solves

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}F^{\ell+1}+v\cdot\nabla F^{\ell+1}=Q_{+}(F^{\ell},F^{\ell})-\nu(F^{\ell})F^{\ell+1},\ \ F|_{t=0}=F_{0},\\
&F^{\ell+1}(t,x,v)=F^{\ell+1}(t,x,R_{x}v)\ \ \ \text{on}\ \ \partial U.\end{split}

From
F_{0}\geq 0
and
F^{\ell}\geq 0
, we have
F^{\ell+1}\geq 0
. ∎

9.
Appendix:
L^{2}
coercivity in an axisymmetric domain

In Appendix, we consider the linearized hard-sphere Boltzmann equation

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{y}f+Lf=g,

(9.1)

with specular boundry condition

\displaystyle f(t,x,R_{y}v)=f(t,y,v)\text{ on }y\in\partial U,

(9.2)

where
R_{y}:=(\mathbf{I}-2n(y)\otimes n(y))
. We consider a periodic cylindrical domain with annulus cross-section:

\displaystyle U:=\Omega\times[0,H]\subset\mathbb{R}^{3},\quad\partial U:=\partial\Omega\times(0,H),

(9.3)

where
\Omega:=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:0<R_{1}<x^{2}+y^{2}<R_{2}\}
is an annulus and
[0,H]
is a periodic interval. It can be parametrized by using a global triply orthogonal coordinate system: for
y\in U
,

\displaystyle y=\eta(\theta,z,r)=(r\cos\theta,r\sin\theta,z)=(\eta_{1}(\theta,z,r),\eta_{2}(\theta,z,r),\eta_{3}(\theta,z,r)),

(9.4)

where
0<R_{1}<r<R_{2},\;0\leq\theta\leq 2\pi,\;0\leq z\leq H
.

The main object of Appendix is to control the physical macroscopic quantities:

\displaystyle a(t,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(t,y,v)\sqrt{\mu}dv,

\displaystyle b(t,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}vf(t,y,v)\sqrt{\mu}dv,

\displaystyle c(t,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}f(t,y,v)\sqrt{\mu}dv.

We provide a constructive proof of the coercivity estimate in the presence of the specular reflection boundary condition. Firstly, we introduce the mass and energy (
a
and
c
) estimates in Proposition
9.5
. On the other hand, when it comes to the momentum component
b
, we use the global triply orthogonal coordinate system (
9.4
) to transform the momentum part such as (
9.9
). Then, we divide it into the tangential momentum and normal momentum. Unlike mass and energy, momentum is not fully conserved, so it needs to be handled using the delicate geometric method. The proof consists of construction of correction term
\zeta
to derive the Laplace-Beltrami operator (
9.24
).

9.1.
Transformed equations and macroscopic parts

In Appendix, we only consider the periodic cylindrical domain with an annulus cross-section. However, Lemma
9.1
, Lemma
9.3
, and Lemma
9.4
in section
9.1
hold for a domain with global triply orthogonal coordinate system of a single chart as well.

Within Appendix, we use variables
(\theta,z,r)
as
x_{1},x_{2}
and
x_{3}
respectively. To distinguish derivatives, we use the following derivative symbols.

\partial_{y_{i}}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}},\quad\partial_{i}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}},\quad\nabla_{y}:=(\partial_{y_{1}},\partial_{y_{2}},\partial_{y_{3}}),\quad\nabla:=(\partial_{1},\partial_{2},\partial_{3}).

We also define

\begin{split}g_{ij}:=\langle\partial_{i}\eta,\partial_{j}\eta\rangle,\quad D_{i}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i},\quad D:=(D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}).\end{split}

(9.5)

In the periodic cylindrical domain with annulus cross-section, it is directly computed that

\displaystyle g_{11}=r^{2},\quad g_{22}=g_{33}=1,\;\text{and}\quad g_{ij}=0,\quad\text{whenever }i\neq j.

(9.6)

We define

\mathbf{v}:=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{v}_{{1}}\\
\mathbf{v}_{{2}}\\
\mathbf{v}_{{3}}\end{bmatrix}=Q^{T}\begin{bmatrix}v_{{1}}\\
v_{{2}}\\
v_{{3}}\end{bmatrix},\quad Q:=\begin{bmatrix}D_{1}\eta_{1}&D_{2}\eta_{1}&D_{3}\eta_{1}\\
D_{1}\eta_{2}&D_{2}\eta_{2}&D_{3}\eta_{2}\\
D_{1}\eta_{3}&D_{2}\eta_{3}&D_{3}\eta_{3}\\
\end{bmatrix},

(9.7)

where
Q
is orthonormal matrix,
QQ^{T}=Q^{T}Q=I
.

Given the global triply orthogonal coordinate system (
9.4
), we define transformed local mass
a_{x}
and local energy
c_{x}
by,

a_{x}:=a\circ\eta\quad\text{and}\quad c_{x}:=c\circ\eta.

(9.8)

For the local momentum vector
b_{x}
, it is crucial to take account of geometry: we define

\begin{split}b_{x_{i}}&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}_{i}f\sqrt{\mu(\mathbf{v})}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}D_{i}\eta\cdot vf\sqrt{\mu(\mathbf{v})}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}=D_{i}\eta\cdot b.\end{split}

(9.9)

Therefore, we get that, as (
9.7
),

\begin{bmatrix}b_{x_{1}}\\
b_{x_{2}}\\
b_{x_{3}}\end{bmatrix}=Q^{T}\begin{bmatrix}b_{{1}}\\
b_{{2}}\\
b_{{3}}\end{bmatrix}.

(9.10)

As the mapping in the velocity space is isometric (see
Q^{T}
is an orthonormal matrix), we note that

|\mathbf{v}|=|v|,\quad|b_{x}|=|b|,\quad\mu=\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{3}}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{3}}}e^{-\frac{|\mathbf{v}|^{2}}{2}}.

(9.11)

In the coordinates, now we rewrite the transport operator in terms of
\mathbf{v}
and local derivatives
\nabla
. Since

\begin{split}\partial_{i}&:=\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}=\sum_{j}\frac{\partial y_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}},\end{split}

we have

\begin{split}\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{1}\\
\partial_{2}\\
\partial_{3}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{g_{11}}&0&0\\
0&\sqrt{g_{22}}&0\\
0&0&\sqrt{g_{33}}\\
\end{bmatrix}Q^{T}\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{y_{1}}\\
\partial_{y_{2}}\\
\partial_{y_{3}}\end{bmatrix},\quad\begin{bmatrix}D_{1}\\
D_{2}\\
D_{3}\end{bmatrix}=Q^{T}\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{y_{1}}\\
\partial_{y_{2}}\\
\partial_{y_{3}}\end{bmatrix}.\end{split}

(9.12)

Therefore, identities (
9.7
) and (
9.12
) yield that

\begin{split}v\cdot\nabla_{y}\ &=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{v}_{1}&\mathbf{v}_{2}&\mathbf{v}_{3}\end{bmatrix}Q^{T}Q\begin{bmatrix}D_{1}\\
D_{2}\\
D_{3}\end{bmatrix}=\mathbf{v}\cdot D{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}.}\end{split}

(9.13)

We also note that, from (
9.7
) and (
9.10
),

\begin{split}b\cdot v&=\begin{bmatrix}b_{x_{1}}&b_{x_{2}}&b_{x_{3}}\end{bmatrix}Q^{T}Q\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{v}_{1}\\
\mathbf{v}_{2}\\
\mathbf{v}_{3}\end{bmatrix}=b_{x}\cdot\mathbf{v}.\end{split}

(9.14)

Together with
a_{x}
and
c_{x}
defined in (
9.8
), we can rewrite the macroscopic part
\mathbf{P}f
as

\begin{split}\mathbf{P}f:=\Big(a_{x}+b_{x}\cdot\mathbf{v}+\frac{|\mathbf{v}|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}.\end{split}

(9.15)

Now we consider some important properties of
D_{i}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i}
. By the normalization in (
9.5
) and (
9.6
),

\langle D_{i}\eta,D_{j}\eta\rangle=\delta_{ij}.{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}}

(9.16)

We define
D_{ij}
and
\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}
by

\begin{split}D_{ij}&:=D_{i}D_{j},\quad\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}:=\langle D_{ij}\eta,D_{k}\eta\rangle.\end{split}

(9.17)

Notice that we calculated the Christoffel symbol in the domain
U

\displaystyle\Gamma_{D,33}^{1}=\Gamma_{D,33}^{2}=0,\quad\Gamma_{D,22}^{1}=\Gamma_{D,22}^{3}=0,\quad\Gamma_{D,11}^{3}=-\frac{1}{r},\quad\Gamma_{D,11}^{2}=0.

(9.18)

Lemma 9.1
.

(1) We have

\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}=-\Gamma_{D,ik}^{j},\quad\Gamma_{D,ij}^{j}=0,\quad\text{for all}\ \ i,j,k\in\{1,2,3\}.\\

(9.19)

(2) We have the following commutator:

\begin{split}D_{i}D_{j}-D_{j}D_{i}=\Gamma_{D,jj}^{i}D_{j}-\Gamma_{D,ii}^{j}D_{i},\quad i\neq j.\end{split}

(9.20)

(3) When all
i,j,k\in\{1,2,3\}
are distinct,

\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}=0.

(9.21)

Proof.

(1) We get (
9.19
) directly from (
9.16
) by taking derivatives.

(2) First we compute that, for
i\neq k
,

\begin{split}\partial_{i}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{kk}}}\big)&=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{kk}}^{3}}\langle\partial_{ik}\eta,\partial_{k}\eta\rangle=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{ii}}{g_{kk}}}\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{kk}g_{ii}}}\partial_{ki}\eta,\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{kk}}}\partial_{k}\eta\rangle\\
&=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{ii}}{g_{kk}}}\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{kk}}}\partial_{k}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i}\eta\big),\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{kk}}}\partial_{k}\eta\rangle\\
&=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{ii}}{g_{kk}}}\langle D_{ki}\eta,D_{k}\eta\rangle=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{ii}}{g_{kk}}}\Gamma_{D,ki}^{k}=\sqrt{\frac{g_{ii}}{g_{kk}}}\Gamma_{D,kk}^{i},\\
\end{split}

(9.22)

where we used (
9.19
) at the last equality. Therefore we conclude (
9.20
) as

\begin{split}D_{i}D_{j}-D_{j}D_{i}&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{jj}}}\partial_{j}\big)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{jj}}}\partial_{j}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i}\big)\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{jj}}}\big)\partial_{j}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{jj}}}\partial_{j}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\big)\partial_{i}\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{jj}}}\Gamma_{D,jj}^{i}\partial_{j}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\Gamma_{D,ii}^{j}\partial_{i}\\
&=\Gamma_{D,jj}^{i}D_{j}-\Gamma_{D,ii}^{j}D_{i}.\\
\end{split}

(3) When
i,j,k
are all distinct, using (
9.19
) and (
9.20
), we derive that

\begin{split}\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}&=-\Gamma_{D,ik}^{j}=-\langle D_{j}\eta,D_{ik}\eta\rangle\\
&=-\langle D_{j}\eta,D_{ki}\eta+\Gamma_{D,kk}^{i}D_{k}\eta-\Gamma_{D,ii}^{k}D_{i}\eta\rangle=-\Gamma_{D,ki}^{j}.\end{split}

Now using above relation successively we derive that

\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}=-\Gamma_{D,ki}^{j}=\Gamma_{D,jk}^{i}=-\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}.

Therefore we conclude (
9.21
).
∎

Definition 9.2
.

Define the Laplacian operator in the normalized
D
-derivatives:

\Delta_{D}:=\big(D_{1}^{2}+D_{2}^{2}+D_{3}^{2}\big).

(9.23)

We also define the Laplace-Beltrami operator in the coordinates:

\Delta_{bel}:=\Delta_{y}=\sum_{i,j}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|g|}}\partial_{i}\big(\sqrt{|g|}g_{ij}\partial_{j}\big).

(9.24)

Lemma 9.3
.

Two operator
\Delta_{D}
and
\Delta_{bel}
have the following relation:

\begin{split}\Delta_{bel}&=\Delta_{D}-\sum_{i}\sum_{k\neq i}\Gamma_{D,kk}^{i}D_{i}.\\
\end{split}

(9.25)

Proof.

Using (
9.1
), we derive that

\begin{split}\Delta_{bel}&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|g|}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{{i}}\big(\sqrt{|g|}\frac{1}{g_{ii}}\partial_{{i}}\big)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|g|}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{{i}}\big(\sqrt{g_{jj}g_{kk}}D_{i}\big)\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{3}D_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{|g|}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\sqrt{g_{kk}}\partial_{x_{i}}\sqrt{g_{jj}}+\sqrt{g_{jj}}\partial_{x_{i}}\sqrt{g_{kk}}\Big)D_{i}\\
&=\sum_{i}D_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\langle\frac{\partial_{ij}\eta}{\sqrt{g_{ii}g_{jj}}},\frac{\partial_{j}\eta}{\sqrt{g_{jj}}}\rangle+\langle\frac{\partial_{ik}\eta}{\sqrt{g_{ii}g_{kk}}},\frac{\partial_{k}\eta}{\sqrt{g_{kk}}}\rangle\Big)D_{i}\\
&=\sum_{i}D_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\langle\frac{1}{g_{jj}}\partial_{j}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i}\eta\big),\frac{\partial_{j}\eta}{\sqrt{g_{jj}}}\rangle+\langle\frac{1}{g_{kk}}\partial_{k}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g_{ii}}}\partial_{i}\eta\big),\frac{\partial_{k}\eta}{\sqrt{g_{kk}}}\rangle\Big)D_{i}\\
&=\sum_{i}D_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\langle D_{ji}\eta,D_{j}\eta\rangle+\langle D_{ki}\eta,D_{k}\eta\rangle\Big)D_{i}\\
&=\Delta_{D}-\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\langle D_{jj}\eta,D_{i}\eta\rangle+\langle D_{kk}\eta,D_{i}\eta\rangle\Big)D_{i}=\Delta_{D}-\sum_{i=1}^{3}\big(\Gamma_{D,jj}^{i}+\Gamma_{D,kk}^{i}\big)D_{i}\\
&=\Delta_{D}-\sum_{i}\sum_{k\neq i}\Gamma_{D,kk}^{i}D_{i}.\end{split}

∎

Lemma 9.4
.

We have the following differentiation identity:

D_{i}\mathbf{v}_{j}=\sum_{k}\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}\mathbf{v}_{k}.

Proof.

From definition (
9.7
),

\begin{split}D_{i}\mathbf{v}_{j}&=D_{i}\langle D_{j}\eta,v\rangle=\langle D_{ij}\eta,v\rangle\\
&=\langle\sum_{k}\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}D_{k}\eta,v\rangle=\sum_{k}\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}\langle D_{k}\eta,v\rangle=\sum_{k}\Gamma_{D,ij}^{k}\mathbf{v}_{k}.\end{split}

∎

9.2.
Mass and Energy estimates

The main purpose of this section is to control macroscopic quantities:

\displaystyle a(t,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(t,y,v)\sqrt{\mu}dv,\quad c(t,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}f(t,y,v)\sqrt{\mu}dv.

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
U
be a domain with global triply orthogonal coordinate system of a single chart. Assume that
f
solves linearized Boltzmann equation (
9.1
) with the specular boundary condition
f(t,y,v)=f(t,y,R_{y}v)
on
y\in\partial U,
where
R_{y}:=(I-2n(y)\otimes n(y))
.
Then we have the following mass and energy estimate:

\begin{split}\|a\|_{2}^{2}&\lesssim\frac{d}{dt}G(t)+\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\\
\|c\|_{2}^{2}&\lesssim\frac{d}{dt}G(t)+\varepsilon\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\quad|G(t)|\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2},\quad\varepsilon\ll 1.\end{split}

(9.26)

Proof.

A starting point of the proof is the weak formulation:

\begin{split}&\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\Psi^{b}(v\cdot n)dAdv-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla_{y})\Psi dydv\\
&=-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Psi\partial_{t}fdydv-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Psi Lfdydv+\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Psi gdydv\\
&\quad+\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{I-P})f(v\cdot\nabla_{y})\Psi dydv.\end{split}

(9.27)

To estimate the mass part
a
, we pick
\Psi_{a}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\phi_{i}(v)\psi_{i}(y)
with

\begin{split}\phi_{i}(v)&=(|v|^{2}-\beta_{a})v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},\\
\psi_{i}(y)&=\partial_{y_{i}}u_{a}.\end{split}

(9.28)

Here,
u_{a}
solves

-\Delta u_{a}=a(x),\quad\partial_{n}u_{a}|_{\partial U}=0,

(9.29)

and the constant
\beta_{a}=10
is chosen so that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|v|^{2}-\beta_{a})(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2})v_{i}^{2}\mu dv=0,\quad i=1,2,3.

(9.30)

Note that the Poisson equation with Neumann boundary condition is uniquely solvable as we have the total mass conservation
\int_{U}ady=0
. When the solution uniquely well-defined, we use the notation

u_{a}=-(-\Delta)_{N}^{-1}a.

(9.31)

For the boundary contribution in (
9.27
), we rewrite the integral in the
x
-coordinate. Using
|\mathbf{v}|=|v|
in (
9.11
) and
v\cdot\nabla_{y}=\mathbf{v}\cdot D
by (
9.13
), we have that

\begin{split}\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\Psi_{a}^{b}(v\cdot n)=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\mathbf{v}_{3}(|\mathbf{v}|^{2}-\beta_{a})\mu\mathbf{v}_{i}D_{i}u_{a}=0.\end{split}

(9.32)

This term always vanishes because when
i=1,2
velocity integration is odd; while when
i=3
the Neumann boundary condition implies
D_{3}u_{a}|_{\partial U}=0
.

For the second term in LHS of (
9.27
), using (
9.30
) and (
9.29
), we derive that

\begin{split}&-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi_{a}\\
&=-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}\Big(a+b\cdot v+c\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi_{a}\\
&=-\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}a{\mu}(|v|^{2}-\beta_{a})v_{i}v_{j}\partial_{y_{j}}^{2}u_{a}dy\\
&=-C\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{U}a\partial_{y_{i}}^{2}u_{a}dy=C\|a\|^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(9.33)

Here,
C=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(|v|^{2}-\beta_{a})v_{i}^{2}dv=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(|v|^{2}-10)v_{i}^{2}dv=-5\neq 0
for all
i=1,2,3.

For the RHS of (
9.27
), from the elliptic estimate to (
9.29
), we use
\|u_{a}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|a\|_{2}^{2}
. Then we could conclude that

\begin{split}&\text{RHS of (\ref {glo IBP re})}+\frac{d}{dt}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\Psi_{a}dydv\\
&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{a}dydv\Big|+\varepsilon\|u_{a}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\lesssim\underbrace{\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{a}dydv\Big|}_{(**)}+\varepsilon\|a\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(9.34)

Here, we set
G(t)=\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\Psi_{a}dydv
which satisfies that
G(t)\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2}
.

To estimate the part
(**)
, we device another test function:

\varphi_{a}=-(-\Delta)^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}a)\sqrt{\mu}.

(9.35)

Note that
(-\Delta)^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}a)
is uniquely well-defined as the mass conservation
\int_{U}a(t)=\int_{U}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{0}\sqrt{\mu}=0
for all
t\geq 0
. When this test function combined with
\partial_{t}f
, we get

\begin{split}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}f\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}a)&=\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}a)\|^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

On the other hand, considering other terms, except
\partial_{t}f
,

\begin{split}\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}a)\|^{2}_{2}&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}a)\Big(-v\cdot\nabla_{y}f+g-Lf\Big)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{P}f+(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f)\ \nabla\cdot\big(v\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}a)\big)\Big|\\
&\quad+\ \Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}a)\big(g-Lf\big)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}a)\|^{2}_{2}+\varepsilon\|a\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}\Big),\\
\end{split}

(9.36)

where we used Poincare inequality and the Young’s inequality with sufficiently small
\varepsilon\ll 1
. Therefore we get

\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}a)\|^{2}_{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\|a\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}\Big).

(9.37)

Using (
9.37
) and
\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\|\cdot\|_{\nu}
, we can estimate
(**)
of (
9.34
) by

\begin{split}\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{a}dydv\Big|&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{a}dydv\Big|+\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{I-P})f\partial_{t}\Psi_{a}dydv\Big|\\
&\lesssim\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}a)\|^{2}_{2}+\|(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\|a\|_{2}^{2}+\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(9.38)

Combining (
9.27
), (
9.32
), (
9.33
), (
9.34
), and (
9.38
), we gain

\begin{split}\|a\|_{2}^{2}&\lesssim\frac{d}{dt}G(t)+\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\quad|G(t)|\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2}.\\
\end{split}

(9.39)

Similar as above mass estimate, in energy
c
estimate, we pick
\Psi_{c}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\phi_{i}(v)\psi_{i}(y)
where,

\begin{split}\phi_{i}(v)&=(|v|^{2}-\beta_{c})v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},\\
\psi_{i}(y)&=\partial_{y_{i}}u_{c},\\
\end{split}

(9.40)

when each are read on
k
-chart and constant
\beta_{c}=5
is chosen so that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|v|^{2}-\beta_{c})v_{i}^{2}\mu dv=0,\quad i=1,2,3,

(9.41)

and
u_{c}
solves,

-\Delta u_{c}=c(y),\quad\partial_{n}u_{c}|_{\partial U}=0.

(9.42)

Note that we have energy conservation
\int_{U}cdy=0
. Similar as (
9.32
), we use oddness integral in
\mathbf{v}_{3}
and Neumann condition of
u_{c}
to get

\begin{split}\iint_{\partial U}f^{b}\Psi_{c}^{b}(v\cdot n)=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\iint f^{b}\mathbf{v}_{3}(|\mathbf{v}|^{2}-\beta_{c})\mu\mathbf{v}_{i}D_{i}u_{c}=0.\end{split}

(9.43)

For transport term of LHS, similar as (
9.33
),

\begin{split}-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi_{c}dydv&=-\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}c{\mu}(|v|^{2}-\beta_{c})\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}v_{i}v_{j}\partial_{y_{j}}^{2}u_{c}dydv\\
&=-C\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{U}c\partial_{y_{i}}^{2}u_{c}dy=C\|c\|^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(9.44)

Here, the constant
C=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(|v|^{2}-\beta_{c})\left(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\right)v_{i}^{2}dv=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(|v|^{2}-5)\left(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\right)v_{i}^{2}dv=5\neq 0
for all
i=1,2,3.

For the RHS of (
9.27
), similar as (
9.34
),

\begin{split}\text{RHS of (\ref {glo IBP re})}&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Psi_{c}\partial_{t}fdydv\Big|+\varepsilon\|u_{c}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\quad\varepsilon\ll 1,\\
&\lesssim\frac{d}{dt}G(t)+\underbrace{\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{c}dydv\Big|}_{(**)}+\varepsilon\|c\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\\
\end{split}

(9.45)

where
|G(t)|\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2}
.
To estimate time part
(**)
, we apply another test function,

\varphi_{c}=\big(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\big)\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}c).

(9.46)

Note that
\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}c)
is well defined by energy conservation. When this test function combined with
\partial_{t}f
,

\begin{split}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}f\big(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\big)\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}c)&=\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}c)\|^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

On the other hand, considering other terms, except
\partial_{t}f
,

\begin{split}\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}c)\|^{2}_{2}&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\big)\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}c)\Big(-v\cdot\nabla_{y}f+g-Lf\Big)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{P}f+(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f)\ \nabla\cdot\big(v\big(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\big)\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}c)\big)\Big|\\
&\quad+\ \Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\big)\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{N}(\partial_{t}c)\big(g-Lf\big)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}c)\|^{2}_{2}+\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|c\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\\
\end{split}

(9.47)

where we used Poincare inequality and sufficiently small
\varepsilon\ll 1
comes from Young’s inequality. Therefore we get

\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}c)\|^{2}_{2}\lesssim\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|c\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}.

(9.48)

Using (
9.48
) and
\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\|\cdot\|_{\nu}
, we can estimate
(**)
by

\begin{split}\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{c}dydv\Big|&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{c}dydv\Big|+\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{I-P})f\partial_{t}\Psi_{c}dydv\Big|\\
&=\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{I-P})f\partial_{t}\Psi_{c}dydv\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\|\nabla\Delta_{N}^{-1}(\partial_{t}c)\|^{2}_{2}+\|(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon\|c\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\end{split}

(9.49)

where we used

\begin{split}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{c}&=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(a+b\cdot v+c\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big){\mu}(|v|^{2}-\beta_{c})v_{i}\partial_{i}u_{c}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}b_{i}{\mu}v_{i}^{2}(|v|^{2}-\beta_{c})\partial_{i}u_{c}=0,\\
\end{split}

(9.50)

because of our choice (
9.41
).

Combining (
9.43
), (
9.44
), (
9.45
), and (
9.49
), we gain

\begin{split}\|c\|_{2}^{2}&\lesssim\frac{d}{dt}G(t)+\varepsilon\|b\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\quad|G(t)|\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2},\quad\varepsilon\ll 1.\\
\end{split}

(9.51)

From (
9.39
) and (
9.51
), we prove Proposition
9.5
.
∎

9.3.
Momentum
b
Estimate

The main difficulty comes from estimating
\|b\|_{2}
. In contrast with the mass and energy estimates, momentum
b
is not entirely conserved and therefore we ought to distinguish the “tangential” momenta and “normal” momentum to the boundary as (
9.9
). In this section we use the global triply orthogonal coordinate system (
9.4
).

For the momentum estimate, we express the weak formulation (
9.27
) in the coordinate system of (
9.4
). Let us use
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
for the domain of
\eta
, namely
\eta^{-1}(U)
. We use a set of test functions
\Psi=\phi(\mathbf{v})\psi(x)
, which depends on the coordinates and transformed velocity (
9.7
). Now we expand interior integral of the LHS of (
9.27
) as

\displaystyle-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi dydv

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j,m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}_{j}\mathbf{v}_{m}\phi(\mathbf{v}){\mu}d\mathbf{v}\Big)b_{x_{m}}D_{j}[\psi(x)]\sqrt{|g|}dx

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j,m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}_{j}\mathbf{v}_{m}D_{j}[\phi(\mathbf{v})]\mu d\mathbf{v}\Big)b_{x_{m}}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx.

(9.52)

For the second term (
9.52
), we use the integration by parts in velocity, (
9.17
), and Lemma
9.4
:

\displaystyle-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi dydv

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j,m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}_{j}\mathbf{v}_{m}\phi(\mathbf{v}){\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}D_{j}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx

(9.53)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,m}\sum_{\ell\neq m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{j}\mathbf{v}_{\ell}{\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}\Gamma^{\ell}_{D,jm}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx

(9.54)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,m}\sum_{\ell\neq j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{m}\mathbf{v}_{\ell}{\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}\Gamma^{\ell}_{D,jj}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx.

(9.55)

Using oddness in velocoity integration, we can expand as following.

\begin{split}&-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi dydv\\
&=-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}^{2}_{j}\phi(\mathbf{v}){\mu}\Big)b_{x_{j}}D_{j}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx\quad(j=m)\\
&\quad-\sum_{j\neq m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}_{j}\mathbf{v}_{m}\phi(\mathbf{v}){\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}D_{j}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx\quad(j\neq m)\\
&\quad+\sum_{j=\ell\neq m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}^{2}_{j}{\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}\Gamma^{j}_{D,jm}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx\quad(j=\ell,\ \ell\neq m)\\
&\quad+\sum_{j\neq\ell,\ell\neq m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{j}\mathbf{v}_{\ell}{\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}\Gamma^{\ell}_{D,jm}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx\quad(j\neq\ell,\ \ell\neq m)\\
&\quad+\sum_{m=\ell\neq j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}^{2}_{m}{\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}\Gamma^{m}_{D,jj}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx\quad(m=\ell,\ \ell\neq j)\\
&\quad+\sum_{m\neq\ell,\ell\neq j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{m}\mathbf{v}_{\ell}{\mu}\Big)b_{x_{m}}\Gamma^{\ell}_{D,jj}\psi(x)\sqrt{|g|}dx.\quad(m\neq\ell,\ \ell\neq j)\\
\end{split}

(9.56)

9.3.1.
Normal momentum
b_{x_{3}}
estimate.

We try three types of test functions, Symmetric, Non-symmetric 1, and Non-symmetric 2.

(Symmetric case)

We try test function
\Psi_{3,1}=\phi_{3}(\mathbf{v})\psi_{3}(x)
, where

\begin{split}\phi_{3}(\mathbf{v})&=(\mathbf{v}^{2}_{3}-\beta_{b})\sqrt{\mu},\quad\text{where}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{v}^{2}_{i}-\beta_{b})\mu d\mathbf{v}=0,\\
\psi_{3}(x)&=\zeta_{3}(x)D_{3}u_{3},\end{split}

(9.57)

where
\zeta_{3}(x)
to be determined and
u_{3}
solves Dirichlet problem,

-\Delta_{bel}u_{3}=b_{x_{3}},\quad u_{3}|_{\partial U}=0.

(9.58)

Above
\phi_{3}
yields only
\mathbf{v}^{2}_{3}\sqrt{\mu}
-terms in (
9.56
). With multiplying proper normalization constants to each
\phi_{i}
’s, LHS of (
9.27
) becomes

\begin{split}&\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\Psi_{3,1}^{b}(v\cdot n)dAdv-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla_{y})\Psi_{3,1}dydv\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\big(\underbrace{b_{x_{3}}\zeta_{3}D^{2}_{3}u_{3}}_{main}+b_{x_{3}}D_{3}\zeta_{3}D_{3}u_{3}\big)\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(-b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{1}_{D,33}-b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{2}_{D,33}+b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,11}+b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,22}\Big)\zeta_{3}D_{3}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\quad+C\int_{x_{3}=0}\Big(\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\phi_{3}(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{3}}_{=0\ \text{oddness in}\ \mathbf{v}_{3}}\Big)\psi_{3}(x)\sqrt{g_{11}g_{22}}dA.\end{split}

(9.59)

(Non-symmetric case 1)

We try test function
\Psi_{3,2}=\sum_{i=1,2}\phi_{i}(\mathbf{v})\psi_{i}(x)
, where

\begin{split}\phi_{i}(\mathbf{v})&=\mathbf{v}_{i}\mathbf{v}_{3}\sqrt{\mu},\\
\psi_{i}(x)&=\zeta_{3}(x)D_{i}u_{3},\end{split}

(9.60)

for
i=1,2
. Above
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
yield only
\mathbf{v}_{3}\mathbf{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu}
and
\mathbf{v}_{2}\mathbf{v}_{3}\sqrt{\mu}
terms in (
9.56
) respectively. With multiplying proper normalization constants to each
\phi_{i}
’s, LHS of (
9.27
) becomes

\begin{split}&\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\Psi_{3,2}^{b}(v\cdot n)dAdv-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla_{y})\Psi_{3,2}dydv\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\big(b_{x_{1}}\zeta_{3}D_{31}u_{3}+b_{x_{1}}D_{3}\zeta_{3}D_{1}u_{3}+b_{x_{3}}\zeta_{3}D_{11}u_{3}+b_{x_{3}}D_{1}\zeta_{3}D_{1}u_{3}\big)\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\big(b_{x_{2}}\zeta D_{32}u_{3}+b_{x_{2}}D_{3}\zeta_{3}D_{2}u_{3}+b_{x_{3}}\zeta_{3}D_{22}u_{3}+b_{x_{3}}D_{2}\zeta_{3}D_{2}u_{3}\big)\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(2b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{1}_{D,33}+2b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,11}+b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{1}_{D,22}+b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,22}+\underbrace{b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,12}+b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{1}_{D,32}}_{=0\ \text{by}\ (\ref {geo cond})}\Big)\zeta_{3}D_{1}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(2b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,22}+2b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{2}_{D,33}+\underbrace{b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,21}+b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{2}_{D,31}}_{=0\ \text{by}\ (\ref {geo cond})}+b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,11}+b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{2}_{D,11}\Big)\zeta_{3}D_{2}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt+C\int_{x_{3}=0}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\phi_{1}(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{3}\Big)\underbrace{\psi_{1}(x)}_{=0}\sqrt{g_{11}g_{22}}dA+C\int_{x_{3}=0}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\phi_{2}(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{3}\Big)\underbrace{\psi_{2}(x)}_{=0}\sqrt{g_{11}g_{22}}dA,\end{split}

(9.61)

where last two terms are zero by Dirichlet boundary condition of
u_{3}
.

(Non-symmetric case 2)

We try test function
\Psi_{3,3}=\sum_{i=1,2}\phi_{i}(\mathbf{v})\psi_{i}(x)
, where

\begin{split}\phi_{i}(\mathbf{v})&=(\mathbf{v}^{2}_{i}-\beta_{b})\sqrt{\mu},\quad\text{where}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{v}^{2}_{i}-\beta_{b})\mu d\mathbf{v}=0,\\
\psi_{i}(x)&=\zeta_{3}(x)D_{3}u_{3},\end{split}

(9.62)

for
i=1,2
. Above
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
yield only
\mathbf{v}^{2}_{1}\sqrt{\mu}
and
\mathbf{v}^{2}_{2}\sqrt{\mu}
terms in (
9.56
) respectively. With multiplying proper normalization constants to each
\phi_{i}
’s, LHS of (
9.27
) becomes

\begin{split}&\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\Psi_{3,3}^{b}(v\cdot n)dAdv-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla_{y})\Psi_{3,3}dydv\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\big(b_{x_{1}}\zeta_{3}D_{13}u_{3}+b_{x_{1}}D_{1}\zeta_{3}D_{3}u_{3}\big)\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\big(b_{x_{2}}\zeta D_{23}u_{3}+b_{x_{2}}D_{2}\zeta_{3}D_{3}u_{3}\big)\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(-b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{2}_{D,11}-b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,11}+b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{1}_{D,22}+b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{1}_{D,33}\Big)\zeta_{3}D_{3}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(-b_{x_{1}}\Gamma^{1}_{D,22}-b_{x_{3}}\Gamma^{3}_{D,22}+b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{2}_{D,11}+b_{x_{2}}\Gamma^{2}_{D,33}\Big)\zeta_{3}D_{3}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&\hskip 8.50012pt+C\int_{x_{3}=0}\Big(\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\phi_{1}(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{3}}_{=0\ \text{oddness in}\ \mathbf{v}_{3}}\Big)\psi_{1}(x)\sqrt{g_{11}g_{22}}dA+C\int_{x_{3}=0}\Big(\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\phi_{2}(\mathbf{v})\mathbf{v}_{3}}_{=0\ \text{oddness in}\ \mathbf{v}_{3}}\Big)\psi_{2}(x)\sqrt{g_{11}g_{22}}dA.\end{split}

(9.63)

9.3.2.
Condition for
\zeta_{3}(x)

We add (
9.59
), (
9.61
), and
(-1)\times
(
9.63
). This is equivalent to applying test function

\Psi_{b,3}:=\Psi_{3,1}+\Psi_{3,2}-\Psi_{3,3},

(9.64)

to (
9.56
). Also using (
9.20
),

\begin{split}&-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi_{b,3}dydv+\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\Psi_{b,3}^{b}(v\cdot n)dAdv\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{3}}\zeta_{3}\big(D_{1}^{2}+D_{2}^{2}+D_{3}^{2}\big)u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{3}}\Big(2\Gamma_{D,33}^{1}\zeta_{3}-D_{1}\zeta_{3}+\Gamma_{D,22}^{1}\zeta_{3}\Big)D_{1}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{3}}\Big(2\Gamma_{D,33}^{2}\zeta_{3}-D_{2}\zeta_{3}+\Gamma_{D,11}^{2}\zeta_{3}\Big)D_{2}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{3}}\Big(2\Gamma_{D,11}^{3}\zeta_{3}+2\Gamma_{D,22}^{3}\zeta_{3}-D_{3}\zeta_{3}\Big)D_{3}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{i}}\Big(-\Gamma_{D,33}^{i}\zeta_{3}+D_{i}\zeta_{3}\Big)D_{3}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\Big(\Gamma_{D,22}^{3}\zeta_{3}+\Gamma_{D,11}^{3}\zeta_{3}-D_{3}\zeta_{3}\Big)b_{x_{i}}D_{i}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx.\end{split}

(9.65)

If we match index
3\rightarrow r,1\rightarrow\theta,2\rightarrow z
and pick

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}=\zeta_{r}=\frac{1}{r}

(9.66)

\zeta_{3}(x)
satisfies

\begin{cases}D_{1}\zeta_{3}&=\Gamma_{D,33}^{1}\zeta_{3}\\
D_{2}\zeta_{3}&=\Gamma_{D,33}^{2}\zeta_{3}\\
D_{3}\zeta_{3}&=\Gamma_{D,11}^{3}\zeta_{3}+\Gamma_{D,22}^{3}\zeta_{3},\\
\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad c\leq|\zeta_{3}|\leq C,

(9.67)

for uniformly positive
c
and
C
due to (
9.4
), (
9.5
) and (
9.18
). Then above system becomes,

\begin{split}&-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f(v\cdot\nabla)\Psi_{b,3}dydv+\iint_{\partial U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{b}\Psi_{b,3}^{b}(v\cdot n)dAdv\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{3}}\zeta_{3}\big(D_{1}^{2}+D_{2}^{2}+D_{3}^{2}\big)u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{3}}\zeta_{3}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{k\neq i}\Gamma_{D,kk}^{i}D_{i}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}b_{x_{3}}\zeta_{3}\Delta_{bel}u_{3}\sqrt{|g|}dx=\int_{U}\zeta_{3}b^{2}_{x_{3}}dy,\end{split}

(9.68)

where we used Lemma
9.3
and (
9.58
).

With
\Psi_{b,3}
, which is defined in (
9.64
), the RHS of (
9.27
) is controlled by

\begin{split}\text{RHS of (\ref {glo IBP re})}&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Psi_{b,3}\partial_{t}fdy\Big|+\varepsilon\|u_{3}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\quad\varepsilon\ll 1.\\
&\lesssim\frac{d}{dt}G(t)+\underbrace{\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{b,3}dy\Big|}_{(**)}+\varepsilon\|b_{x_{3}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\\
\end{split}

(9.69)

where
|G(t)|\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{2}^{2}
. Here, we used
\|u_{3}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|b_{x_{3}}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\|\cdot\|_{\nu}
.

To estimate time part
(**)
, we apply another test function,

\varphi_{3}=\mathbf{v}_{3}\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{D}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}.

(9.70)

When this test function combined with
\partial_{t}f
, we get

\begin{split}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}f\mathbf{v}_{3}\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{D}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}&=\|\nabla\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\|^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

On the other hand, considering other terms, except
\partial_{t}f
,

\begin{split}\|\nabla\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\|^{2}_{2}&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}_{3}\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{D}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\Big(-v\cdot\nabla f+g-Lf\Big)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{P}f+(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f)\ \nabla\cdot\big(v\mathbf{v}_{3}\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{D}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\big)\Big|\\
&\quad+\ \Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}_{3}\sqrt{\mu}\Delta^{-1}_{D}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\big(g-Lf\big)\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\|\nabla\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\|^{2}_{2}+\Big(\|a\|_{2}^{2}+\|c\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}\Big),\\
\end{split}

(9.71)

from Poincare inequality and sufficiently small
\varepsilon\ll 1
comes from Young’s inequality. Therefore we get

\|\nabla\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\|^{2}_{2}\lesssim\|a\|_{2}^{2}+\|c\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2}.

(9.72)

Using (
9.72
), we can estimate
(**)
in (
9.69
) by

\begin{split}\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{b,3}dy\Big|&\lesssim\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{b,3}dy\Big|+\Big|\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{I-P})f\partial_{t}\Psi_{b,3}dy\Big|\\
&\lesssim\big(\|c\|_{2}\|\nabla\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\|_{2}\big)+\big(\varepsilon\|\nabla\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\|^{2}_{2}+\|(\mathbf{I-P})f\|_{2}^{2}\big)\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\|a\|_{2}^{2}+\|c\|_{2}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\nu}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{2}^{2}+\|g\|_{2}^{2},\end{split}

(9.73)

where we used

\begin{split}&\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{b,3}\\
&=\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{3,1}+\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{3,2}-\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{P}f\partial_{t}\Psi_{3,3}\\
&\leq\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(a+b\cdot v+c\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}(\mathbf{v}_{3}^{2}-\beta_{b})\sqrt{\mu}\zeta_{3}D_{3}\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\\
&\quad+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(a+b_{x}\cdot\mathbf{v}+c\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}(\mathbf{v}_{i}\mathbf{v}_{3})\sqrt{\mu}\zeta_{3}D_{3}\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}}_{=0\ \text{odd in velocity}}\\
&\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\iint_{U\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(a+b\cdot v+c\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}(\mathbf{v}_{i}^{2}-\beta_{b})\sqrt{\mu}\zeta_{3}D_{3}\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\\
&\lesssim\|c\|_{2}\|\nabla\Delta_{D}^{-1}\partial_{t}b_{x_{3}}\|_{2}\\
